METHOD AND FINDINGS · 3 NOVEMBER 2026

How the finder works

We identify English property assessments that may qualify for expanded full small business rates relief from 1 April 2027. A cautious main list is accompanied by a separate list of cases needing review.

HOW TO INTERPRET THE RESULTS

This is an automatic screen using recorded property information. It does not establish the current occupier, an actual relief award or whether a bill will newly become £0. “Likely” is a screening category, not a measured probability. Sector mappings remain draft.

SourcesProcessFindingsSCAT classificationsLimits and council checksReproduce and correct the work

The sources and dates

Inputs used for this published snapshot
SourceVersion and purpose
VOA rating list2026 list entries, epoch 0004 baseline. File dated 1 October 2026; latest record date 29 September. Supplies the original assessment, address, premises description, firm name and rateable value.
ONS postcode directoryAugust 2026 directory, using 2024 Westminster constituencies. Supplies exact postcode geography.
Budget announcement28 October 2026 announcement; source page updated 30 October. Supplies the April 2027 policy assumptions. This release models the announcement and marks it as not yet in regulations.
Official RHL guidanceGuidance updated 30 March 2026 informs premises-use mapping. Final April 2027 rules and actual circumstances govern entitlement.
Government council directoryRetrieved 3 November 2026. Supplies official council homepages, matched to the VOA billing-authority code.
Independent Earley and Woodley workbook64 assessment references and 61 non-empty supplied trading-name entries. Used for comparison and identification; names are labelled unverified.

This is a fixed snapshot. The website does not automatically refresh property data, names or policy rules.

Our five processing steps

  1. Read the original assessment. We preserve the VOA property reference, address, firm name, description and rateable value. Deleted-property proxy records with no rateable value are set aside with reasons. Missing postcodes are retained. One assessment is not necessarily one business.
  2. Select the value band. The target is above £12,000 and up to £18,000 inclusive, in English billing authorities. Exactly £12,000 and values above £18,000 fall outside this particular full-relief expansion target. Rateable value is an assessment value, not the annual bill.
  3. Screen recorded use. Specific qualifying premises descriptions without detected conflicts enter the likely list. Specific SCAT categories for convenience stores, takeaways and hair/beauty activities can resolve generic shop wording, but cannot override uncertain mappings or conflicting evidence. Generic shops without specific activity evidence, conditional uses and possible exclusions need review. Explicitly coded supporting offices or stockrooms do not by themselves make recognised qualifying shop/cafe uses uncertain. Clearly non-qualifying recorded uses are outside target. Recreational marinas can qualify; unspecified marinas need review.
  4. Match location and council. We assign an English constituency only by an exact postcode match. Terminated-postcode matches need review. There is no postcode-sector approximation. The billing council comes from the VOA authority code, not the constituency. Unassigned review cases have their own download.
  5. Keep identity concerns visible. Firm-name matches flag possible multiple occupation without proving a common ratepayer. Missing names do not establish sole-property occupation and do not cause review by themselves. Supplied trading names show their source, import date and unverified status; they do not independently establish eligibility.

Original VOA firm names remain available. A separately supplied trading name may identify a site, owner or operator rather than the legal ratepayer. Specific property-use concerns are recorded separately with evidence.

Understanding the review categories

Review records carry separate flags for possible exclusion, conditional use, generic shop activity, uncertain sector, location, possible common ratepayer and property-specific evidence. Flags can overlap. The use assessment distinguishes clear recorded use with location/ratepayer warnings from uncertain activity. These are screening categories, not statistical confidence intervals.

What the screening produced

National results for the 3 November 2026 publication
OutcomeAssessmentsHow to use it
Likely candidates26,446The default constituency view. Recorded type fits the target with no detected screening conflict.
Possible candidates, assigned88,799A separate view with a reason for each concern. These remain possible candidates requiring checks.
Possible candidates, unassigned181Retained in a separate download because no exact English constituency match was available.

The constituency files contain 115,245 candidate and review assessments across 543 English constituencies. Another 124,737 English assessments in the value band are outside target on recorded use. “Outside target” does not establish that no relief of any kind is available.

The constituency CSV/Excel links contain both lists. Use Download filtered CSV for the selected view and filters. The original modelled eligibility/status fields remain in exports for comparison; they are not awards. We do not calculate actual savings or newly zero bills.

National screening report (JSON) · Unassigned review records (CSV)

SCAT classifications and reasoning

Category labels come from VOA specification Appendix 2. The sector assignments and reasoning below are our interpretation of the recorded types, informed by the official RHL guidance linked above.

This is the complete list of SCAT codes in our reference/mapping tables and those observed in the VOA snapshot. These are our draft classifier interpretations, not official relief awards. Expand a code to see its primary-code rules, mapped sector, confidence, rationale and review status. Search includes the explanations inside each row.

A SCAT does not always have one classification. Text flags take priority, then primary+SCAT overrides, then SCAT-only overrides, then the primary-code fallback. Listed primary associations come from the mapping tables and observed records, not a restriction on other combinations. The * row explains other primary codes; the full primary-code download supplies their fallback rules.

“Other” means outside mapped RHL, not necessarily a statutory exclusion. Medium/low mapping confidence, generic shops and conditional activities require more evidence. Even high-confidence sector mappings still pass through property-level screening.

Text flags can override code mapping

  • possible vape shop: No VOA code identifies vape shops. Coverage is very small. The 2027 definition of a vape shop is not yet published. Fields: description_text;firm_name;number_or_name; confidence: low; review: awaiting_review. Pattern: \bVAPES?\b|\bVAPING\b|\bE-?CIG.
  • possible adult gaming centre: Description text names an arcade or gaming centre. The 2027 definition of an adult gaming centre is not yet published. Fields: description_text; confidence: medium; review: awaiting_review. Pattern: ADULT GAMING|GAMING CENTRE|GAMING ARCADE|GAMING LOUNGE|AMUSEMENT ARCADE|\bAGC\b.
  • leisure: Recreational dance lessons, SI 2025/1093 Sch 2 para 11. Dance schools are often coded EP (school) under unrelated SCAT codes. Fields: description_text; confidence: medium; review: awaiting_review. Pattern: \bDANCE (SCHOOL|STUDIOS?|CENTRE|ACADEMY)\b.

What still needs checking

Why 543 constituencies, not 650

The finder covers England only. It includes all 543 English Westminster constituencies on the 2024 boundaries. The other 107 constituencies are in Scotland (57), Wales (32) and Northern Ireland (18), and they are left out on purpose.

Business rates are devolved. The relief expansion modelled here was announced for England and applies to English billing authorities. The Scottish Government, Welsh Government and Northern Ireland Executive each set their own reliefs, with different thresholds and rules, so applying the English policy to their properties would give wrong answers. The data also differs: the VOA rating list we use covers England and Wales, while Scottish properties are valued by Scottish assessors and Northern Ireland properties by Land & Property Services.

Welsh assessments appear in the VOA file but are set aside for that reason. Extending the finder to the rest of the UK would need each nation's own relief rules and, for Scotland and Northern Ireland, different source data.

The screening release passed 157 Python tests, eight Node tests and consistency checks on every published constituency row. Browser checks included an actual filtered download. These check software behaviour and data consistency; they do not measure the proportion of businesses that really qualify.

We have not completed a representative council-verified audit, established all current uses or ratepayers, or confirmed actual relief receipt. Recorded descriptions and supplied names can be stale. Sector mappings remain draft. A property may already have a zero bill because of another relief.

Before making a definitive statement, confirm the current occupier and ratepayer, qualifying use, other properties, applicable rules and effective date, existing reliefs and council award/bill. To establish a newly zero bill, also establish the previous amount payable and why it changes.

Council links are directory-sourced homepages, sometimes for successor authorities; not every endpoint has been individually availability-tested. Use the site's business-rates section. A link is a route to confirmation, not confirmation of any property.

WORDING FOR AN UNVERIFIED CASE

“This property may qualify for expanded relief. Please check your circumstances with your council.”

These properties appear likely to qualify for expanded full small business rates relief based on their rateable value and recorded premises use. The data does not confirm the current occupier, their other properties, existing reliefs or final bill. Before telling a business that it will have nothing to pay, confirm its circumstances with the linked billing council.

Reproduce, share and correct the work

The original CampaignLab repository contains the code, versioned rules/mappings, explicitly published outputs and a research record. Original source ZIPs and intermediate files are not committed. Source and policy updates require an explicit rebuild.

  • Detailed methodology and sources
  • Reproduction steps and release checks
  • Download field reference
  • How to propose an evidenced correction

VOA data uses its own restricted terms, not the Open Government Licence. Retain provider notices, source dates, name provenance and the screening disclaimer when sharing extracts. Read the VOA terms and our data-use and attribution notes. No VOA endorsement or blanket clearance for downstream uses is claimed.
